3. ACCOMMODATION FOR EXHIBITS.
★ The Exhibition may be arranged and decorated as
the exhibitor wishes, insofar as the display will not screen
light from others. The highest point of the exhibit shall
not exceed over 15 feet (some 4.5 meters) above the
ground.
★ The exhibitor is requested to design the decoration
which may harmonize with the interior of the building.
the‘ensemble' or the immediate environment.
The standard measures of the equipment for exhibi-
tion above the ground floor are as follows:
1. Exhibiting step—5 inches (some 13 centimenters).
2. The base-line of the exhibit in the show-case -20
inches (some 50 centimeters).
3. The top of the railing 26 inches (some 0.66 meter).
4. The height of the top of the show-case-from 12 to
15 feet (sone 3.6 meters -some 4.5 meters).
★ The height of railing shall be uniform, and for the
railing along the passages, the floor shall be taken as
the baseline.
-12 -

=== Page 273 === (confidence 0.99)
★ The placard and tag for the exhibits will be sent to
the exhibitor from the Exhibition Committee, and the
exhibitor has to fill the blanks as specified, and attach
them to the exhibits when they are shipped.
When the exhibitor has notified the Exhibition office
of the shipment of the cargo, carriage paid, by sending
the bill of lading, together with the necessary original
documents, the Exhibition Committee will take the neces-
sary steps, to transport exhibits and equipment materials
to the Exhibition grounds.
★ Exhibitors are requested to send all exhibits to the
Port of Yokohama.
Landing-charges, custom duties and carriage from
Yokohama to the Exhibition grounds, or to the storehouse,
will be dealt with by the Exhibition authorities at lowest
possible rates.
★ The exhibitor is requested to unpack and display the
articles himself, or through his agent.
Empty boxes shall be carried out of the Exhibition
grounds, and kept in the storehouse named by the Exhibi-
tion office.
When the Exhibition comes to the end, exhibitors
shall pack up all articles within a fixed period, and hand
them over to the Exhibition office for return.
★ All exhibits shall necessarily arrive at the Exhibition
grounds not later than the month of January, 1933.
-14-

=== Page 274 === (confidence 0.99)
5. HOURS OPEN TO THE PUBLIC.
★ The Exhibition will be open from 8 a. m. until 5 p.
m. every day.
In case of need such as in an emergency, however,
an extension or shortening of time may be arranged by
the Exhibition authorities.
6. CLEANING OF THE SHOW-ROOM.
★ It is the duty of the exhibitor, or of his agent, to
clean the show-room, to dust exhibits and remove waste
to the designated places after the Exhibition is closed
cach day. Should the exhibitor fails in cle ning the
show-room in his share, the Exhibition ofice may arrange
to do so at his expense.
7. OBSTRUCTION OF PASSAGES.
★ The exhibitor and his employees shall not obstruct
the passages or hinder inspection by visitors during the
Exhibition hours.
8. FIRE OR LIGHTING.
★ In the Exhibition building. the use of fire, or of any
lighting other than that provided by the contract with
the Exhibition authorities, is prohibited.
9. MATERIALS NEEDED FOR EXHIBITS.
★ When the exhibitor desires to remove materials used
for the production or demonstration of the exhibit, as
well as manufactured articles or waste products, he shall
obtain first permission from the Exhibition authorities, as
also a certiticate of permission to effect the removal.
-15-

=== Page 275 === (confidence 0.99)
10. PROTECTION AND SUPERVISION OF EXHIBITS.
★ For the guarding of the Exhibition grounds, and the
supervision of the exhibits, the Exhibition authorities
provide guards and watchmen, but the exhibitors may
hire special watchmen for the protection of their exhibits,
if they may deem necessary. All these watchmen shall
be appointed by the Exhibition authorities.
★ To protect the exhibits from dust or rain, the ex-
hibitors should place cover of suitable materials on them
while the Exhibition is closed.
11. THE EXHIBITOR'S AGENT.
★ The one who is in charge of the articles is taken to
be the exhibitor's agent, and when the exhibitor is absent,
it is this agent's duty to pursue the regulations or instruc-
tions of the Exhibition on the exhibitor's part.
12. ADMITTANCE.
★ The exhibitor and his employees are admitted to the
Exhibition grounds on showing the exhibitor's admission
card or the gate-pass specially issued to them.
13. DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS FOR ADVER-
TISEMENT.
★ It is not permitted to distribute in the show-rooms or
grounds any materials for advertisment, except those
approved of by the Exhibition authorities.
14. PROHIBITED USE OF SPACE FOR EXHIBITION.
★ The exhibitor is not allowed to display, sell or
advertise anything other than those he produces or deals
-16-

=== Page 276 === (confidence 0.99)
with. Also he shall not sell 'articles exhibited unless
permission is first obtained from the Exhibition authorities.
15. EXHIBITING NOISY DEVICES.
★ If it be desired to display noisy devices, permission
will only be granted after some adequate silencer has
been applied to them.
16. WITHDRAWAL OF EXHIBITS OR CLOSING OF
THE SHOW-ROOM.
The exhibitor is not allowed to withdraw the exhibits
or to close their stands or show-room so long as the
Exhibition remains open.
★ Immediately after the final closing of the Exhibition,
the exhibitor shall begin the withdrawal of both exhibits
and equipment and to complete removal within one
week.
When this work is not completed within the specified
time, the exhibits or materials, as well as the equipments,
may be considered as abandoned, and the Exhibition
authorities may give instruction to remove them, or
dispose of them in any way considered suitable.
17. SALE OF EXHIBITS.
★ Exhibits may be sold on the terms that they are
delivered upon the closing of the Exhibition; and only in
case the exhibitor obtains the approval of the Exhibition
authorities, he may be allowed to sell and deliver them
on the spot of the Exhibition. Such articles are limited
to those produced there by way of demonstration.
-17-

=== Page 277 === (confidence 0.99)
18. DISTRIBUTION OF HANDBILLS, SOUVENIERS,
ETC.
★ The distribution of handbills, souveniers, etc. is for-
bidden in the Exhibition grounds, at the entrances of the
show-rooms or in the passages, etc.
19. PHOTOGRAPHING THE SHOW-ROOM OR EX-
HIBITS.
★ When the exhibitor wishes to photograph the show-
room or the exhibits, he shall first obtain the permission
of the Exhibition authorities.
20. SUPPLEMENTARY RULES.
★ The Exhibition may provide special rules for the
supervision of the Exhibition grounds, and all exhibitors
shall have the duty to observe the rules of the Exhibition.
★ Other items not included in this regulations, shall
be provided for by the regulations specially established.
★ Should the exhibitor wish to make any complaint to
the head of any section, he may petition it to the Presi-
dent by writing, but while the petition is being discussed
the decision of the section head remains valid.
★ The Exhibition reserves the right to refuse those
exhibits which do not agree with the object of the
Exhibition, as also the right of rejecting from, or refusing
to admit to, the Exhibition, any exhibitor, his agent or
employee, who violates the regulations, and who give
trouble to spectators or other exhibitors.
★ These regulations apply not only during the opening
of the Exhibition, but also before the opening and after
the closing.

DEPARTMENT OF STATE
DIVISION OF FAR EASTERN AFFAIRS
July 34, 1934.
Suggutiono
I am favorably disposed to the albjeot
that there be American participation in the
Yokohama Municipal Exposition, which is
disoussed in Yokohama's despatch No. 21 of
July 5, 1934, largely for the reason that we
cannot expect to have the Japanese participate
in American expositions, of which so many
are held, unless the courtesy is occasionally
returned.
It will be noted that the Mayor of
Yokohama states that this Government will
be informed of the exposition being held,
through the usual channes. It is my
opinion that no action should be taken on
the basis of the present despatch until
such notification has been received. We
shall then be in position to know the exact
status of the exposition, that is, whether
it is to be undertaken by the Japanese
Government in collaboration with the
municipality of Yokohama, or whether it is
purely a private venture of the municipality.

CHILDREN'S INTERNATIONAL
The Honorable
The Secretary of State,
Washington.
Sir :
With reference to the Embassy's despatch No. 78
of August 5, 1932, and the Department's reply No. 1o2
of October 8, 1932 (file No. 894.607), concerning a
Women's and Children's International Exhibition to be
held in Tokyo from March 17 to May 10, 1933, I wish
MAY 1 9 1933
FILED
to inform the Department that in a series of special
"days"
1C

=== Page 314 === (confidence 0.99)
- 2 -
"days" arranged by the Exhibition in honor of the va-
rious nations, the American Day was celebrated first,
on March 19, two days after the official opening of the
Exhibition. On that occasion, which I attended with Mr.
Neville, Counselor of the Embassy, the American flag was
flown at the mast in the center of the Exhibition
grounds, while another large American flag, presented
by me at the request of the Exhibition authorities, was
suspended from the gallery of the hall where the exer-
cises were held and attended by a guard of honor of four
Japanese boy scouts. On the playing of "The Star Spangled
Banner" by the orchestra, the entire audience who packed
all available space in the large hall and gallery, arose
and waved small American flags, ending with a burst of
genuine applause. At the close of my address, of which
1/.
a copy is appended, I presented to the Exhibition - at
the request of its officers - two American dolls dressed
in costumes of the period of George Washington which had
been made in the United States, intended for exhibition
with similar dolls from the other nations. The speech,
together with an interpretation in Japanese, was broad-
casted by radio throughout Japan.
In this connection it is reported to the Embassy by
the Exhibition authorities that a collection of various
articles from America forwarded by Ambassador Debuchi
arrived at the Foreign Office a few days ago and have
already been received at the Exhibition.
Confidential.
In case part of this despatch should be released by
the

=== Page 315 === (confidence 0.99)
-3 -
the Department to the press, I add confidentially and
not for publication, that the demonstration of the large
audience, which was composed of middle and lower class
Japanese, in honor of the United States, impressed me as
unusually enthusiastic and friendly in character. Of course,
the national characteristics of politeness and hospitality
entered into it: I was their guest and they wished to make
me feel at home. At the same time, the thought crossed
my mind that it might perhaps be just as easy for the au-
thorities, if they wished it, to stage a demonstration
of hostility to the United States with the same or a similar
audience. In other words, the effect produced would depend
upon the preparations made. The Japanese people are like
sheep and easily led; they are an excitable race and react
readily to emotional stimuli. With American flags waving
and the band playing "The Star Spangled Banner", they de-
monstrated for the United States; might they not as readily
have demonstrated against the United States if stimulated
by anti-American speeches and the waving of Japanese flags?
Perhaps so, but I am inclined to believe that the enthusiasm
for the United States comes more or less naturally, while
hostility toward the United States requires artifical stimu-
lation.
At any rate, the incident demonstrated clearly to me
the interest which the Japanese people hold toward our
country. I doubt if any Japanese crowd would exhibit as
much enthusiasm, either friendly or unfriendly, toward any
other country as I saw them exhibit toward the United States
at

=== Page 316 === (confidence 0.95)
-4-
at this meeting. The United States to them is very
evidently of greater interest and importance than any
other country in the world. If only it were possible to
suppress the anti-American propaganda of the military
element and of the ultra-patriotic societies, and to
direct the interest of the people into channels of
friendly cooperation with the United States, there need
never be any thought of hostile sentiment in Japan.
Respectfully yours,
Toon
Joseph C. Grew

CONGRATULATORY MESSAGE AT THE AMERICAN DAY EXERCISES
SPOWSORED BY THE WOMEN S AND CHILDREN'B INTERNATIONAL
EXHIBITION, March 19, 1933.
It 1s a distinguished privilege to be present today
as the guest of the Women's snd Children's International
Exhibition and to fulfill with the brief words I shall
say to you, two pleasant duties incumbent upon me. Ae-
cording to the provisional progrem which wes sent to me
some time ego, the address of the Americen Ambass dor 1s
to be a "Congratulatory Message". And so 1t is, and a
very hearty one. But were I to stop there I should not
be discharging a second duty which I am sure all Americans
here present feel very patently, nemely to render cordial
thanks to the officials of the Exhibition for their hos-
pitable, friendly action in organizing this American Day.
It is a gesture of friendliness and cooperetion which
my countrymen and I greatly ap reciate, e gesture in keep-
1ng with the broed human principles which prompted the
Kosel-ka1 (Association for the Study of Incustrial Policy)
and the Dai-Nihon Rengo Fujin-kai (Japenese Federstion of
Women's Socleties) to carry through their undertaking to
the splendid fruition which we see around us today in Ueno
and in Shibaura.
In congratulating you, however, on this splendid
achievement I am reminded of the basic objectives of the

=== Page 318 === (confidence 0.98)
promoters. These objectives are three in number, and, if

 I have the honor to enclose herewith obpies of
AI
corrempondence I have recently had with the mayor of
Yokohama, relating to the proposed Yokohama Municipal
Expomition which will be held in this city from March
24 to May 26, 1935. A self-explanatory sketoh-map
and photograph are also enclosed.
It will be noted that in the finar paragraph of
AUG 8 - 1934
bts totter the mayor requests that the Government of
FILED
the United States be informed of the Exposition, and
requests its "aid with moral support in this projeot."
Subsequent to the receipt of that letter, two members
of the mayor's staff called at the Consulate and in-
timated that exhibits from the United States would be
welcomed. This had not been mentioned in the letter
as final arrangements with the Yokohama Customs for
the free entry of exhibits had not been completed.
This privilege having been granted, except for products
offered

=== Page 323 === (confidence 0.99)
- 2 -
offered for sale, there remains no obstacle for the
exhibition of goods from abroad. Exhibitors must pay
all freight and other charges, and are required to
pay for space at nominal rates, to be announced later.
In addition to seeking exhibits from abroad, it
was learned from interviews with competent officials
of the City Office that "moral support in this project"
is supposed to mean assistance in stimulating tourists
to visit Yokohama during the Exposi tion.
A copy of my reply to the mayor is also enclosed.
While I informed him that I would communicate the pur-
port of his letter to the Department, I also suggested
that requests for exhibits and tourist propaganda should
more properly emanate from the Japanese Embassy in
Washington, or some Japanese consular office in the
United States. As I have orally been informed that
this will be done, it is likely that in due course the
Department will receive communications in the premises
from the Japanese Embassy.
As will be seen from the accompanying map and
photograph, it is proposed to hold the Exposition in
the park on the water-front immediately opposite the
Consulate; and as the vacant lot next to the office will
also be used, the Exposition will enclose the consular
premises on two sides. As traffic on the main road in
front will undoubtedly be heavy, it is thought that it
will be necessary to close to two front gates of the
Consulate, more to discourage loiterers than anything
else. There is a side entrance which can be used without
groat

=== Page 324 === (confidence 0.97)
- 3 -
great inconvenience. Special police protection will
be readily granted if deemed necessary.
I have been informed that plans will be practi-
cally complete by the end of September, l934, and
that construction will commence immediately thereafter.
As soon as details are available, I shall not fail to
make a full report to the Department.
In the meantime, it is respectfully urged that
in the interests of goodwill and amity between the
United States and Japan, American firms having in-
terests in the Far East might very appropriately be
informed of the Exposition. All inquiries thereanent
should be addressed to the Yokohama City Office,
Yokohama, Japan.

American Consulate.

Sir:
I have the pleasure to inform you that early
next Spring the City of Yokohame, under the auspices
of the Yokohama Municipality will hold an Exposition
in commemoration of the reconstruction of the City
and great progress it has made since it rose from
the ashes of its destruction in an earthquake and
fire of September lst, 1923, and also, in addition,
as an expression of thanks and appreciation for the
kind sympathy and help extended to us by the people
of your country at the time of disaster.
This Exposition has also been planned with the
idea of promoting and encouraging home industries
and foreign trade, through the development of better
international economic relations, and this feature
of the projeot, the various products of the Empire
will be brought together and exhibi ted in attractive
buildings.
The Bxposition will be held from March 24th to
May 26th, 1935, and during the season when our
country

=== Page 326 === (confidence 0.97)
C O E Y
- 2 -
country is noted for its cherry blossoms, which are
greatly admired for their beauty.
Yamashita-Park and its immediate vicinity has
been ohosen as the site, where the beauty of the
flowers and green trees along the seashores are always
attractive to visitors and will forn a pleasing
contragt to the harmonized exhibition buildings that
are being planned.
The transportation facilities can be secured
easily on land and sea to the exposition and the
fullness of exhibit, as well as the accomodation for
comfort and requirements, and management for every
need will be arranged so carefully.
This occasion will present a rare opportunity
to visitors from your country to attend this Exposition
and at the same time to study general conditions as they
exist in Japan today.
I wish partioularly therefore to solicit your kind
assistance and shall be deeply grateful if you will
inform your Government and country of this Exposition
and its underlying purpose and request them to aid us
with moral support in this projeot.
I have the honour to be,
Sir,
I. Ohnishi (signed)

=== Page 337 === (confidence 1.00)
Prospectus of The Grand Yokohama Exposition in
Commemoration of its Reconstruction 1935
Since Yokohama was devastated by the great earthquake
and its attendant conflagration on September 1st, 1923, it has
made remarkable progress in the process of reconstruction due
to the great aid received from foreign countries, the government,
and the untiring efforts of its citizens.
As a result, Yokohama has become a great modern city with
fine new government, public and private buildings, wide paved
streets and efficient public services.
To maintain its position as the castern gateway to the
Japanese Empire, Yokohama has greatly improved its harbour
facilities in the past few years. The harbour has been improved,
modern piers erected, sheds constructed and transportation
facilities extended. These improvements have made it possible
for her to more than adequately meet the requirements of
discharging the tons of merchandise to and from all parts of
the world with which she has to cope daily.
The municipality has reclaimed some 600,000 tsubo of land
along the waterfront, and large factories of numerous industries
are now being erected on this advantageous site.
In consequence, the population of Yokohama, which was
under 450,000 before the 1923 catastrophe, is now approximately
700,000.

=== Page 338 === (confidence 0.99)
To commemorate the reconstruction of Yokohama, and to
promote the growth of her industries, the municipality has
decided to hold an exposition next spring. This plan will serve
to introduce the present thriving conditions existing here to
people at home and abroad, to judge and compare foreign and
Japanese manufactures, and also to strengthen the economic ties
between Japan and forcign nations.
It is the aim of the municipality to introduce to the public
the products of industries and commercial activities of organiza-
tions established in Yokohama, and to aid their progress, and
at the same time to promote prosperity in this country and
abroad.
The City of Yokohama hopes for the support of not only
the people in Japan but other nations as well, for the realiza-
tion of the purpose of the exposition.
The municipality requests the public to concur with the
plans of the exposition authorities and to cooperate with them
by participating in the exposition.
President of the Grand Yokohama Exposition
in Commemoration of its Reconstruction, 1935
Ichiro Ohnishi,
Mayor of Yokohama.

Chairman of the Yokohama
Municipal Assembly.

Vice President of the Yoko-
hama Chamber of Commerce
and Industry.

INFORMATION AND REGULATIONS OF
THE EXPOSITION.
General Information.
1. The object of the exposition is to commemorate the
reconstruction of Yokohama, to promote trade and
industry by the exhibition of materials and products at
home and from abroad.
2. The exposition is named the Grand Yokohama Exposi-
tion in Commemoration of its Reconstruction, 1935, and
it will be held under the auspices of the Yokohama
Municipality.
3.
The site of the exposition will be Yamashita Park and
the adjacent area in Yamashita-cho.
The exposition will be held from March 26th to May
24th, 1935.
The business office of the exposition is in the Yokohama
Municipal Office. It will be moved to the site of the
exposition at a later date if deemed necessary.
6.
An outline of the various types of exhibits to be
displayed at the exposition are the following:
a) Manufacturing Industry.
b) Chemical Industry.
c) Mechanical and Electrical Industries.
d) Agricultural, Forestry, Marine and Mining Industrics.
e) Patents and Inventions.

Rules Regarding Exhibits.
2. Exhibits which arrive at the site of the exhibition before
the official opening, and which are not to be exhibited
before that date will be taken care of by exhibition officials
until that time. It is requested that all exhibits be removed
from the grounds within five days after the esposition
closes.
3. Exhibitors displaying motors should take special care in seeing
that these exhibits have substantial foundation and that noises
and vibrations from them are reduced to a minimum. Also,
precautions should be taken that overheating and leakages do
not cecur.
13

=== Page 350 === (confidence 0.98)
4. Exhibits damaged or soiled in any way must be removed
immediately and replaced by duplicates Eshibiters of
perishable goods must remove then eshibits at the dite
specified by the exposition suthorities
5. When exhibitors wish to change the price of any of their
exhibits during the esposition, they must apply for permis-
sion to do so from the exposition authorities
6. Exhibits for sale at the exposition will be limited to those
which can be replaced by duplicate eshibits Sales made
are to be reported to the exposition authorities as soon as
they are completed.
7. Exhibits at the exposition are not insured by the esposition
authorities, and exhibitor: who wish to insure their exhibits
must do so at their own expense.
8. In case exhibitors desire to take their exhibits from the
exposition they must first reccive the permission of the
exposition authorities before doing so.
Apprication should be made to the Esposition Office,
Yokohama City Office.

AMERICAN CONSULATE
Yokohama, Japan, Apr11 18, 1935.
OF
74416
MAY -6 35
MAY 8-1934
UEPT.
STAIE
SUBJECT:
The Grand Yokohama Exposition.
OF
1-1055
2D
PAR
THE HONORABLE
THE SECRETARY OF STATE
894.607 YOKOHAMA/14
WASHINGTON
SIR:
I have the honor to refer to despatches No. 21
and 38 of July 5 and August 22, 1934 regarding the
Yokohama Exposition commemorating the opening of the
port after the great earthquake of l923. The Exposition
was formally opened, according to schedule on March 27,
1935. I have to enclose herewith the special supple-
ment of the Japan Advertiser of April 7, 1935, regarding
the Exposition.
As is usual with expositions, neither all the
buildings nor all the exhibits were completed upon the
FILED
10 1935
The sponsors of the Exposition made an effort to
interest several cities on the Pacific coast of the
United States to send exhibits, but the attempt was
not successful. It is believed that the Exposition
was not planned sufficiently ahead of time to induce
FFG
foreigners

=== Page 365 === (confidence 0.98)
- 2 -
foreigners to take part in it. It was not organized
on the seale of the Century of Progress to begin with
and there was considerable opposition among Yokohama
interests. All Yokohama business firms were called upon
to contribute towards the expenses and the American
companies made heavy contributions along with all other
foreign firms.
Bxhibits
The Exposi tion is predominantly a Japanese industrial
and commercial display. There is very little in it beyond
the advertising exhibits of Japanese manufacturers and
retail houses. ▲ tour of the business district of Tokyo,
including the large department stores, would produce the
same impression as a tour of the Exposition. There are
no interesting or unusual scien tific or artistic exhibits.
The impression of the visitor is that the Exposi tion is
"Buy Japanese" propaganda.
There are very few foreign exhibits. There are
three American exhibitors: one is a phonograph company
with a factory in Yokohama. The company's exhibit is a
small separate building with a huge loud-speaker on top
which plays alternately, Japanese and Occidental music,
all day long. An American rubber tire factory in Yoko-
hama and an American electrical equipment company in
Tokyo have exhibits in the main buildings. Brazil has
a

=== Page 366 === (confidence 0.97)
- 3 -
a separate building with a display of its products,
emphasizing its coffee production. Siam has a building,
decorative outside, with very little of interest inside.
Nestle's condensed milk company has a amall building where
its product is served. The Canadian Pacific Steamship
Company and the Canadian National Railways have separate
booths in one of the large buildings. The Soviet tourist
organization has a booth in one of the buildings. While
there are a few other features, described below, the
Exposition is of purely local interest.
Various parts of Japan are represented by buildings
with oxhibits of their products. For example, the island
of Hokkaido has two buildings. Nagoya, Chosen and Formosa
have separate buildings. "Manchukuo" has a building. As
manufacturers and retail companies have exhibits, separate
from the displays put up by the administrative sections of
Japan, there is much duplication.
The Japanese Army and Navy have separate buildings
with elaborate displays illustrating modern warfare on
land and sea. Miniature stages show hypothetical naval
engagements and rather realistically portray some of Japan's
recent land operations. Large tables showing the strength
of Japan's military and naval equipment as compared with that
of foreign nations are given considerable prominence.
Huisance Aspect
As stated in the previous despatohes and as showm in
the diagrams enelosed with those despatahes, the consulate
18

=== Page 367 === (confidence 0.97)
- 4 -
is located in the midst of the Exposition. The Bund, the
street upon which the consulate is located, as well as
the Yokohama United Club, the Standard Vacuum Oil Company,
the Hong Kong Shanghai Bank and the Hotel New Grand, is
closed to motor traffic during the day. Along the opposite
side of the street from the beginning of the Bund to the
Hotel New Grand is a continuous line of shops or stalls,
facing the onsulate and the other buildings. On good
days the Bund is crowded with people. Naturally a great
deal of trash blows into the consular compound. The
three front gates of the premises are kept closed, to
keep Exposition visitors from wandering inside. All
gates are locked at night.
▲aross the narrow street at the side of the consulate,
facing the principal officer's office windows, is a small
restaurant, inside the Exposition grounds, and about ten
yards to the left is a public toilet, of the primitive
trench design. As soon as warm weather sets in the flies
and odors from the kitchen and the toilet may become a
serious nuisance. I have already spoken to the city
authorities regarding the matter, but aside from putting
up an additional wooden fence to shut off the view of the
toilet the authorities do not seem to understand what
further objection there can be.
Ammeements
The chier attraction is the so-called "American
Rodeo". The arena is in the open. The mhow includes a
number

=== Page 368 === (confidence 0.97)
- 5 -
number of acts, all imported from the United States: -
(a) a "wild West" aot consisting of four cowboys, two
cowgirls, aud four Indians; (b) a motorcyole act with
about six performers; (c) a high diving act with two
performers; (d) a man with a trained chimpanzee and a
trained lion; and (e) a knife throwing act. As Yokohama
has unfortunately been suffering very wet weather since
the Exposition opened, the American entertainment has not
been a paying proposition. The Exposition authorities are
reluotant to pay the performers for rainy days. This office
took up the complaints of the actors with success thus far.
On good days the show is fairly well attended.
There is also a theater, in which Japanese dancing
and movies are given.
Duration of the Exposition
It has been intimated that although the Exposition
is supposed to close on May 24th it is more than likely
that it will carry on for several months longer. Thus
far the extremely wet weather has been a great handicap
to the Exposition and it is said that the City has already
run Yen 80,000 behind their emtimated receipts.
Respectfully yours,
Rroyce
Richard F. Boyeo,
American Conmúl.
